Second, the source test: does the outlet exist, is it older than the story, does its own homepage carry the news it is supposedly breaking? Third, the quote test — fabrications lean on emotional direct quotes with no author, no place and no time. And increasingly, the eyes test: a deepfake video or cloned voice now demands the same scepticism that photoshopped images earned a decade ago, and the tools that detect them are improving exactly as fast as the tools that make them, which is to say: it is a race, and readers are the track.
  - **第 5 段**:The habit that changed us most, though, was not defensive but social: we appointed my niece "chief sceptic", with a formal right — exercised constantly, with relish — to reply to any dramatic claim with "source?" and to be thanked, not resented, for it. Something psychological shifted once checking became a role rather than an insult. Corrections stopped being embarrassing; forwarding slowed visibly; and the chat developed a new genre, the pre-emptive debunk, where one of us posts a rumour already annotated with its verdict. The paywall problem, I should admit, sometimes slows us down — a story locked behind a subscription resists the quick check — but headlines, dates and bylines are free, and they carry most of the tell-tale cargo.
  - **第 6 段**:Media literacy, I have concluded after our family's small humiliation, is not a syllabus to complete but a set of reflexes to install, and reflexes are built by repetition in low-stakes moments, ideally before the high-stakes ones arrive dressed as news. The actor lives. The hoax site has since published forty more deaths, three miraculous cures and one royal baby. Our chat now checks first and mourns later, which sounds colder and is actually kinder: sympathy wasted on fiction is sympathy withheld from the real world. The niece, for her part, has added the phrase to her biography on every platform she owns: "I ask for sources. It's not personal."
- 词汇表:
  - misinformation:错误信息
  - screenshot:截图
  - rumour:传闻;谣言
  - echo chamber:信息茧房;回音室
  - algorithm:推荐算法
  - fact-check:事实核查
  - deepfake:深度伪造
  - paywall:付费墙
  - media literacy:媒介素养
- 理解题:
  - **Q1(r3-13-q1)** What made the fake death story spread so effectively in the family chat?
    - A. A professional video
    - B. Emotional fuel plus the unclickable authority of a screenshot
    - C. The actor confirmed it
    - D. It came from a newspaper
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:传播靠的是情感燃料 + 截图"无法点击、看似权威"的特性。
  - **Q2(r3-13-q2)** What did the family's "autopsy" conclude about misinformation?
    - A. It relies on advanced technology
    - B. It fools people through feelings arriving faster than habits
    - C. Only old people fall for it
    - D. It is always political
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:解剖结论:错误信息"不是靠技术骗人,而是靠比习惯先到的情绪"。
  - **Q3(r3-13-q3)** How does the writer describe the echo chamber?
    - A. A cage that traps everyone identically
    - B. A hall of mirrors where the same lie wears an outfit tailored by your own history
    - C. A political tool
    - D. A chat feature
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:回音室不是笼子,而是"由你自己的历史量身裁衣的同一谎言"—— halls of mirrors。
  - **Q4(r3-13-q4)** What is the "screenshot test" in the family's toolbox?
    - A. Ask the sender directly
    - B. If it cannot be clicked, search a few keywords plus hoax or a fact-checking site
    - C. Never open screenshots
    - D. Forward it to police
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:截图测试:不可点击就必须检索——"关键词 + hoax 或事实核查网站"。
  - **Q5(r3-13-q5)** Why did appointing a "chief sceptic" change the chat's behaviour?
    - A. It made checking a role rather than an insult
    - B. It silenced the niece
    - C. It reduced messages to zero
    - D. It monetised the chat
    - 答案:A
    - 解析:一旦"核查"变成角色而不是冒犯,纠错不再尴尬、转发明显变慢。
  - **Q6(r3-13-q6)** What does the writer say about sympathy wasted on fiction?
    - A. It is harmless
    - B. It is sympathy withheld from the real world
    - C. It builds community
    - D. It helps actors
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:结尾:"浪费在虚构上的同情,就是从真实世界克扣下的同情"。

## 第 14 篇 · r3-14:Two Million Views, Eleven Pounds: A Year Inside the Creator Economy

- 主题:媒体网络 | 级别:B2 | 词数:702
- 摘要:从厨房手机拍摄到百万播放:一位半职视频博主关于流量算法、变现、赞助与倦怠的诚实账本。
- 正文:
  - **第 1 段**:I started the channel for the least glamorous reason available: my sourdough starter was overflowing and I needed somewhere to put the enthusiasm. Two years later I have two million cumulative views, forty thousand subscribers, an inbox that sorts itself into brands, scams and fellow bakers, and a monthly income that would not cover my rent in a city I would not want to live in anyway. This is the honest ledger nobody films: the creator economy looks like a lottery from the outside and feels like a smallholding from the inside — endless labour, modest yields, occasional weather.
  - **第 2 段**:The first thing the algorithm taught me is that it is not a wizard but a matchmaker. Videos succeed when they are watched, saved and shared, and it measures those signals with unsentimental precision. My breakthrough was accidental: a livestream of a failed bake, left running out of laziness, outperformed every polished upload I had agonised over, because people could write comments in real time and I could laugh at the disaster in real time. Since then I have structured everything around the comment section as a venue rather than an afterthought: asking genuine questions in the first minute, pinning the best reply, letting the audience steer the next episode. The lesson is unglamorous but repeatable — retention is a relationship, not a hook.
  - **第 3 段**:Monetisation arrived in tiers, each with its own psychology. Advertising revenue was the first and smallest: eleven pounds in my opening month, a sum so comic I framed the statement. Affiliate links did better, because baking equipment is something people genuinely buy, and honesty turned out to be the strategy — the single highest-earning video I ever made was titled "Do not buy the expensive one", which is not a sentence traditional marketing would survive. A newsletter became the stable spine of the income: people who will follow a recipe across inboxes will follow it anywhere, and an email list, unlike a follower count, cannot be taken away by a redesign.
  - **第 4 段**:Sponsorship taught me the hardest lessons. The first offer arrived with flattering speed and a contract written in a language I did not yet speak; I signed it, and spent a fortnight promoting a flour brand I did not use, watching my own comment section cool towards me with every upload. It paid three months of groceries and cost something harder to price. The rules I extracted, expensively: only promote what you already buy; say the price of your integrity out loud and then add VAT; and disclose everything, always, not because regulations require it (they do) but because audiences forgive commerce and punish disguise. A good sponsorship now feels less like an advertisement and more like a collab — the brand sends the ingredient, the audience gets the recipe, and the video would exist anyway.
  - **第 5 段**:The costs that surprised me were not financial. Binge-watch culture expects presence in bulk, and the publishing treadmill — edit on Tuesday, film on Thursday, answer comments forever — quietly colonises the week. My burnout arrived not as exhaustion but as numbness: filming a loaf I did not care about, in a kitchen I had stopped seeing, for an audience I had started to fear disappointing. The recovery was structural, not spiritual: fewer videos, prepared in monthly batches; a co-editor paid properly; two comment sessions a week instead of a permanent vigil; and a private account, followed by eleven people, where I post bakes that exist only because I wanted to eat them. The channel survived the retreat, which taught me the year's most valuable lesson: the audience was never asking for volume. The metrics were.
  - **第 6 段**:Would I recommend the smallholding? To anyone who wants a craft with a public, absolutely; to anyone who wants a salary, not yet, and possibly never. The two million views were not a jackpot but a slow compounding of usefulness. The eleven pounds is framed and hanging in the kitchen, next to the first loaf photograph and the printout of the first kind comment from a stranger in Chile. Keep the ledger honest, price your integrity with VAT, batch your labour, protect the private joy — and let the algorithm matchmake while you, contentedly, keep baking.
- 词汇表:
  - content creator:内容创作者
  - algorithm:算法
  - livestream:直播
  - monetise:变现
  - newsletter:电子邮件通讯
  - sponsorship:赞助
  - collab:合作(共创内容)
  - binge-watch:刷剧;连续观看
  - burnout:倦怠
- 理解题:
  - **Q1(r3-14-q1)** Why did the writer start the channel?
    - A. To make money fast
    - B. They needed somewhere to put sourdough enthusiasm
    - C. A brand hired them
    - D. Friends dared them
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:最不性感的理由:酸面团酵种漫出来了,热情需要去处。
  - **Q2(r3-14-q2)** What did the writer learn from the accidental livestream success?
    - A. Longer videos always win
    - B. The comment section is a venue — retention is a relationship, not a hook
    - C. Livestreams are free money
    - D. Polish is everything
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:事故直播胜过所有精修视频:把评论区当现场,留存是关系而非钩子。
  - **Q3(r3-14-q3)** Which video earned the most affiliate income, and why?
    - A. A review of the most expensive tools
    - B. "Do not buy the expensive one" — honesty turned out to be the strategy
    - C. A sponsored flour ad
    - D. A silent baking video
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:标题"别买贵的那款"的视频赚得最多——诚实本身就是策略。
  - **Q4(r3-14-q4)** What rules did the writer extract from the bad sponsorship?
    - A. Only promote what you already buy, price your integrity and disclose everything
    - B. Never take sponsorships
    - C. Always accept the first offer
    - D. Hide disclosures in descriptions
    - 答案:A
    - 解析:昂贵的教训:只推自己在用的、给标价加"增值税"、永远披露。
  - **Q5(r3-14-q5)** What form did the writer's burnout take?
    - A. Sleeping all day
    - B. Numbness — filming loaves they did not care about
    - C. Fear of cameras
    - D. Anger at comments
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:倦怠不是疲惫而是麻木:拍自己不在乎的面包,给害怕失望的观众。
  - **Q6(r3-14-q6)** What did retreating from volume teach the writer about the audience?
    - A. The audience was never asking for volume — the metrics were
    - B. Viewers want daily uploads
    - C. Subscribers leave quickly
    - D. Comments are worthless
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:收缩后频道安然无恙:"观众从来没要求产量,要产量的是数据"。

## 第 15 篇 · r3-15:Reading the Bills: A Household Energy Audit

- 主题:生活服务 | 级别:B2 | 词数:711
- 摘要:看懂水电账单的每一行:固定费用、费率、直接借记、智能电表与一间老房子的保温改造,省钱的都是笨办法。
- 正文:
  - **第 1 段**:The envelope arrived with its monthly menace, and for years I had paid it the way one pays weather: as a fact beyond negotiation. Then a friend, over dinner, asked the question that rebooted my finances: "Do you actually know what you're paying for?" I did not. I knew the total, feared the winter, and could not name a single line item. So one Sunday I made tea, opened a year's worth of bills, and performed the least glamorous and most profitable investigation of my adult life: a household energy audit, conducted entirely from the kitchen table.
  - **第 2 段**:The anatomy of the bill was the first shock. The headline number is not the price of energy; it is a bundle. There is the standing charge — a daily fee for the privilege of being connected, charged before a single watt flows — and then the tariff, the per-unit price, usually split into day and night rates on older meters. There were also, historically, estimated readings rather than actual ones, which had quietly overcharged me for two winter months and undercharged me for a summer. The arithmetic lesson is simple and applies to every bill in the house: totals persuade, line items confess. Anyone trying to cut costs who reads only the total is polishing the windscreen of a car with no engine.
  - **第 3 段**:Armed with the anatomy, the fixes sorted themselves by dignity, from least to most effortful. The meter reading, once submitted monthly by phone app, killed the estimation problem instantly. Switching to a direct debit smoothed the seasonal panic — the same amount monthly, settle up annually — though I learned to check the settlement month like a hawk, because providers set payment plans with the optimism of insurers. Then came the standing charge comparison, where the audit earned its keep: two suppliers with identical tariffs differed by eleven pence a day on the charge, which is forty pounds a year for the same product. Switching took eleven minutes online and a two-week cooling-off period I did not need.
  - **第 4 段**:The smart meter changed the relationship further. Within a fortnight of installation, the in-home display had taught me more about my own habits than a decade of bills: the kettle's cost per boil, the oven's warm-up spike, the mysterious overnight draw that turned out to be an old freezer in the garage, working honest shifts for a family of three. We did not become monk-like. We simply gave the freezer early retirement, batched the baking, and moved the washing to the cheap-rate window, three behavioural changes worth roughly a month's bill each year. Information, it turns out, does not automatically change behaviour, but displayed information in the hallway, glowing accusingly, has a better record than good intentions.
  - **第 5 段**:The deepest layer was the house itself. A thermal camera borrowed from the local library — a service I recommend everyone discover — photographed our heat leaving in technicolour: a loft hatch leaking like a chimney, a door frame whistling gently, a wall cavity performing no cavity duties whatsoever. The loft insulation was topped up in a weekend for less than the cost of a games console; draught-proofing took a day and a mastic gun. The camera is now a party trick. The house, which once required the heating's full enthusiasm by November, now holds its warmth like a different building entirely, and the audit's final chart shows the same rooms, the same weather, and a bill twenty-three per cent lower than the year before.
  - **第 6 段**:What did the great energy audit teach me? That utility bills are designed to be paid, not read, and that this is a choice available for reversing. That the boring levers — a monthly reading, a direct debit reviewed, a standing charge compared, a loft topped up — move more money than any amount of lifestyle sacrifice. And that the system, whatever its reputation, responds briskly to a customer who arrives knowing the names of the line items. The same Sunday audit has since been repeated on the broadband, the water and the phone contract, each with findings. The envelope still arrives with its monthly menace. I now open it the way an auditor opens a good book: slowly, with a pencil, and with the quiet expectation of finding something.
- 词汇表:
  - standing charge:固定日均费用
  - tariff:费率
  - meter reading:抄表读数
  - direct debit:直接借记(定期代扣)
  - smart meter:智能电表
  - loft insulation:阁楼保温层
  - bill:账单
  - cooling-off period:冷静期
  - council tax:市政税
- 理解题:
  - **Q1(r3-15-q1)** What triggered the writer's household energy audit?
    - A. A price warning letter
    - B. A friend asking whether they knew what they were paying for
    - C. A power cut
    - D. A news article
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:朋友一句"你到底知道自己在为什么付钱吗"重启了作者的财务生活。
  - **Q2(r3-15-q2)** What did the writer learn about bill totals and line items?
    - A. Totals tell the whole story
    - B. Totals persuade, line items confess
    - C. Line items are fake
    - D. Totals are always wrong
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:账单第一课:"总额在说服你,明细在招供"。
  - **Q3(r3-15-q3)** How did the writer save forty pounds a year for the same product?
    - A. By using less hot water
    - B. Two suppliers had identical tariffs but a daily standing-charge gap of eleven pence
    - C. By switching to candles
    - D. By buying a new fridge
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:两家供应商费率相同,固定日均费差 11 便士——一年就是 40 镑,换供应商只需 11 分钟。
  - **Q4(r3-15-q4)** What effect did the smart meter's in-home display have?
    - A. None at all
    - B. It exposed habits — like the old garage freezer's overnight draw — prompting three cheap behavioural changes
    - C. It doubled the bills
    - D. It broke the heating
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:室内显示器像"发光的问责":找到车库旧冰柜的夜间耗电,三个小改变每年省出一个月账单。
  - **Q5(r3-15-q5)** What did the borrowed thermal camera reveal?
    - A. Nothing unusual
    - B. Heat escaping through the loft hatch, door frame and an underperforming cavity
    - C. The roof was perfect
    - D. The floor was the problem
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:图书馆借来的热成像仪拍出"彩色漏热图":阁楼口像烟囱、门框漏风、墙腔毫无作为。
  - **Q6(r3-15-q6)** What is the writer's closing attitude to the monthly bill?
    - A. Fear and avoidance
    - B. Opening it slowly, with a pencil, expecting to find something
    - C. Paying it unread forever
    - D. Framing it on the wall
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:如今开账单像审计师翻开好书:"慢慢地、带着铅笔、期待有所发现"。

## 第 16 篇 · r3-16:The Library Card That Replaced a Thousand Subscriptions

- 主题:生活服务 | 级别:B2 | 词数:722
- 摘要:一张免费借书卡能做什么:从阅览室、档案库到志愿者、食物银行与投票站,重新发现社区公共服务的说明书。
- 正文:
  - **第 1 段**:I signed up for a library card to save nine pounds on a paperback, and I write this as a confession, because the card then quietly reorganised my entire relationship with the town I had lived in for six years without belonging to. The library, it turns out, is not a building full of books. It is the last public indoor space where nobody is expected to spend money, and once you walk in without your wallet's permission being required, you start noticing what else the town has been offering for free while you were paying subscriptions for inferior versions of it.
  - **第 2 段**:The books came first, obviously, and in volume: my borrowing history now runs to four paperbacks a month, plus the surprise instruments of a modern membership — e-books, audiobooks and streaming through an app that would cost more annually than my electricity. But the deeper discovery was the reading room, a silence so complete and so collectively maintained that it feels like a civic monument. In that room I have sat beside students, jobseekers polishing applications, an elderly man reading every newspaper in order, and a new parent rocking a pram in the only warm, quiet place available to her at ten in the morning. The reading room is the town thinking.
  - **第 3 段**:Then the card opened doors I did not know it unlocked. The local history archive, two floors down, holds maps, photographs and council records that predate the building itself; the archivist, when I asked about my street, produced a photograph of it in 1908, horse-drawn and treeless, and a renewal ledger showing the library had been lending through two wars and a pandemic. The reservation system ships any book in the county network to my local branch within days, which makes my "to-read" pile someone else's logistics problem. There are warm banks in winter, a repair café on Saturdays, and a printed list of every club that meets under this one roof: chess, knitting, genealogy, a writing circle whose average age drops by a decade every time I attend.
  - **第 4 段**:Somewhere along the way, the library turned me into a participant rather than a customer. I volunteer two mornings a month at the digital help desk, where the queue is patiently endless: pensioners wrestling password resets, a fisherman uploading insurance photos, my own neighbour discovering that the device her children gave her is not, in fact, beyond learning. The library also runs the town's food bank collection point, and stacking tins between the poetry shelves recalibrates what you think a public service is for. On election mornings the same building becomes a polling station, the quiet room filling with pencils on strings and a queue of people who have all, somehow, found the one place in town designed to receive them.
  - **第 5 段**:The civic immunity I have developed since is worth itemising. When the council proposed cutting library hours, the objection I wrote cited usage statistics the librarians printed for me — footfall, job applications supported, children's summer reading — and the hours survived, which taught me that local services respond to evidence delivered in the language of decisions. I have reported fly-tipping through the council app and watched it vanish within a week; filed a noise complaint about the bakery's 3 a.m. exhaust fan, resolved by an amicable fan upgrade rather than a feud; and learned that my council tax, that unloved monthly line item, is precisely what pays for the warm room, the archivist and the pencils. Democracy, it turns out, has a branch office, and it opens at nine.
  - **第 6 段**:The economics still make me laugh. Nine pounds saved on a paperback; thousands saved since on books I borrowed, software licences the library replaced, a co-working desk I no longer rent, and the coffee I do not buy while working in a room that asks nothing of me. But the real return is harder to bank: I know my neighbours' faces now, and the archivist's name, and which librarian reads every detective novel in order of acquisition. A town is not a place on a map; it is a set of rooms where your presence is expected. I carry the card in the wallet's front slot, where the money used to go first, and I have never once, since the day I signed up, felt poorer for it.
- 词汇表:
  - library card:借书证
  - reading room:阅览室
  - archive:档案库
  - renewal:续借
  - overdue:逾期(未还)
  - volunteering:志愿服务
  - food bank:食物银行
  - polling station:投票站
  - fly-tipping:非法倾倒垃圾
  - council tax:市政税
- 理解题:
  - **Q1(r3-16-q1)** Why did the writer first get a library card?
    - A. To save nine pounds on a paperback
    - B. For the free wifi
    - C. A friend recommended it
    - D. For job hunting
    - 答案:A
    - 解析:办卡的起因很"功利":省 9 镑买一本平装书。
  - **Q2(r3-16-q2)** What does the writer mean by "the reading room is the town thinking"?
    - A. It is very quiet
    - B. It hosts students, jobseekers, readers and parents — the whole town in one shared silence
    - C. Only scholars may enter
    - D. It has free newspapers
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:阅览室里有学生、求职者、读报的老人、摇婴儿车的父母——"这就是小镇在思考"。
  - **Q3(r3-16-q3)** What hidden services did the card unlock?
    - A. Only e-books
    - B. The local history archive, county-wide reservations, repair café, warm banks and clubs
    - C. A private study room
    - D. Discounted cinema tickets
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:借书卡还解锁了地方档案库、全县预约、修理咖啡馆、取暖银行和各类俱乐部。
  - **Q4(r3-16-q4)** What did volunteering at the digital help desk teach the writer?
    - A. That old devices cannot be fixed
    - B. That neighbours and strangers keep arriving with teachable problems, including the writer's own neighbour
    - C. That libraries should charge fees
    - D. That wifi is unreliable
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:数字帮帮台的队伍"耐心地无穷无尽",连作者自己的邻居都在其中——设备并非学不会。
  - **Q5(r3-16-q5)** How were the library's opening hours saved from cuts?
    - A. A petition with no evidence
    - B. An objection citing usage statistics printed by the librarians, in the language of decisions
    - C. A protest march
    - D. The mayor intervened
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:作者用馆员打印的使用数据(人流、求职、暑期阅读)写反对信——"用决策的语言递交证据"。
  - **Q6(r3-16-q6)** What does the writer call the library at the end of the passage?
    - A. A free bookstore
    - B. A branch office of democracy, open at nine
    - C. A museum
    - D. A subscription service
    - 答案:B
    - 解析:结论句:"民主原来有分支机构,九点开门"——图书馆就是它的营业厅。

## 第 47 篇 · r3-047:节日:我的看法(festivals: My View)

- 主题:节日文化 | 级别:B2 | 词数:714
- 摘要:一篇关于节日文化的议论文:由年货市场谈起,层层论证并给出建议。

    - D. n. 小巷,胡同

    - 解析:“alley”(B1)意为:n. 小巷,胡同。
  - **Q7(r3-115-q7)** 从文中可以推断出这次旅行的整体感受是?

    - 解析:作者整体论证理性积极,可推断:愉快且值得。
  - **Q8(r3-115-q8)** 作者写这篇文章的主要目的是?

    - 答案:B
    - 解析:议论文常见目的:记录一次旅行的经历。
